NOTICE TO MEMBERS
Notice is hereby given that the 27thAnnual General Meeting 
(AGM) of the Members of PNC Infratech Limited  will be held 
on Wednesday, September 30, 2026 at 11:30 A.M. (IST) 
through Video Conferencing (“VC”)/ Other Audio-Visual 
Means (“OAVM”) (“hereinafter referred to as “electronic 
mode”) to transact the following business:
ORDINARY BUSINESS:
1. To receive, consider and adopt –
a) The audited Standalone Financial Statements of 
the Company for the financial year ended March 
31, 2026, along with the report of the Board of 
Directors and Auditors thereon;
b) The audited Consolidated Financial Statements of 
the Company for the financial year ended March 
31, 2026, along with the report of the Auditors 
thereon.
2. To declare dividend on equity shares for the financial 
year ended March 31, 2026.
3. To appoint a Director in place of Mr. Yogesh Jain, 
Managing Director, (DIN: 00086811) who retires 
by rotation and being eligible, offers himself for re-
appointment.
4. To appoint a Director in place of Mr. Anil Kumar Rao, 
Whole Time Director, (DIN: 01224525) who retires 
by rotation and being eligible, offers himself for re-
appointment.
5. APPOINTMENT OF M/s. NSBP & COMPANY, 
CHARTERED ACCOUNTANTS, NEW DELHI, FIRM 
REGISTRATION NO. 001075N, AS THE STATUTORY 
AUDITORS OF THE COMPANY
 To consider and if thought fit, to pass with or without 
modification(s), the following resolution as an 
ORDINARY RESOLUTION:  
 “RESOLVED THAT  pursuant to the provisions of 
Sections 139, 141, 142 and other applicable provisions, 
if any, of the Companies Act, 2013 read with the 
Rules framed thereunder as amended from time to 
time (including any statutory modification(s) or re-
enactment thereof for the time being in force) and based on the recommendation of Audit Committee and 
the Board of Directors of the Company, M/s. NSBP & 
Company, Chartered Accountants, New Delhi, Firm 
Registration No. 001075N be and is hereby appointed 
as the Statutory Auditors of the Company, to hold 
office for a term of two consecutive years from the 
conclusion of the 27th Annual General Meeting (AGM) 
until the conclusion of the 29th AGM of the Company, 
on such remuneration as may be mutually agreed 
upon between the Board of Directors and the Statutory 
Auditors. 
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT  the Board of Directors of 
the Company (including any Committee thereof) be 
and is hereby authorized to do all such acts, deeds, 
matters and things, including execution of necessary 
documents, writings and instruments, filing of requisite 
forms and returns with the Registrar of Companies and 
other statutory or regulatory authorities, and to take all 
such steps as may be necessary, proper or expedient to 
give effect to this Resolution and matters incidental or 
ancillary thereto. ”
SPECIAL BUSINESS 
6. RATIFICATION OF REMUNERATION PAYABLE TO M/S. 
GAURAV JAIN & ASSOCIATES, COST ACCOUNTANT , 
COST AUDITOR OF THE COMPANY
 To consider and if thought fit, to pass with or without 
modification(s) the following resolution as ORDINARY 
RESOLUTION :
 “RESOLVED THAT  pursuant to the provisions of

the Companies Act, 2013 read with Companies (Audit

1,00,000/- Per Annum (Rupees one lakh only) plus 
applicable taxes thereon and reimbursement of out-
of-pocket expenses on actual basis, to M/s. Gaurav 
Jain & Associates, Cost Accountant (Firm Registration 
Number – 004160 ) Cost Auditors of the Company 
to conduct the audit of cost records of the Company 
for the financial year ended March 31, 2027 be and is 
hereby confirmed, ratified and approved.

7. REAPPOINTMENT OF MR. PRADEEP KUMAR JAIN, 
CHAIRMAN & MANAGING DIRECTOR (DIN: 00086653)
 To consider and if thought fit, to pass with or without 
modification(s), the following resolution as SPECIAL 
RESOLUTION: 
 “RESOLVED THAT pursuant to provisions of section 
196, 197, 198 and 203 of the Companies Act, 2013 
read with Schedule V of the Companies Act, 2013 and 
the other applicable provisions of the Companies Act, 
2013 and the rules and regulations made thereunder 
and other applicable provisions of the SEBI (Listing 
Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 
2015 (including any statutory modifications or re-
enactment thereof, for the time being in force), and 
relevant provisions of the Articles of Association of 
the Company, as amended from time to time, and 
based on the recommendation of the Nomination and 
Remuneration Committee and Board of Directors of the 
Company, consent of the members be and is hereby 
accorded for the reappointment of Mr. Pradeep Kumar 
Jain (DIN: 00086653) as a Chairman & Managing 
Director of the Company for a period of 5 years 
commencing from October 1, 2026 and for continuation 
of his office as Chairman and Managing Director upon 
his attaining the age of 70 years during the tenure of 
his appointment, i.e., on or after August 27, 2027, on the 
terms and conditions including remuneration as set out 
below:
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT Mr. Pradeep Kumar Jain, 
Chairman & Managing Director shall be entitled to 
remuneration by way of Salary, commission, Dearness 
Allowance, Perquisites and other allowances as follows:
 Salary:
 Upto ` 502.44 Lakhs (Rupee Five Hundred Two Lakh 
Forty-Four Thousand) per annum.
 Perquisites: Mr. Pradeep Kumar Jain shall be entitled 
to perquisites within the limits as specified below: 
•	 Rent free accommodation or house rent 
allowance upto of 40% of salary. Expenses 
pertaining to electricity will be borne/ reimbursed 
by the company. The Company shall provide such 
furniture, fixture and furnishing as approved by the 
Nomination and Remuneration Committee of the 
company from time to time.•	 Reimbursement of medical expenses incurred 
for self and family upto one months’ salary in a 
year or three months’ salary over a period of three 
years.
•	 Leave travel concession for self and family once a 
year in accordance with the rules of the Company. 
•	 Personal accident insurance, the premium for 
which shall be paid by the Company.
•	 Club fees for a maximum of two clubs (excluding 
admission fees and life membership fees).
•	 Provident fund, superannuation benefits and 
gratuity as per the rules of the Company, 
subject to applicable guidelines for managerial 
remuneration.
•	 Provision of chauffeur Driven Company’s car 
(including all maintenance expenses).
•	 Provision of telephone facility at residence.
•	 Encashment of leave at the end of tenure in 
accordance with the rules of the Company.
 Explanation:  Perquisites shall be evaluated as per 
the provisions of the Income-tax Rules, wherever 
applicable. In the absence of any such rule, perquisites 
shall be evaluated at actual cost to the Company.
 Commission:
 In addition to the aforesaid remuneration, Mr. Pradeep 
Kumar Jain shall be entitled to commission within the 
limits prescribed under Section 197 of the Companies 
Act, 2013, being up to 5% of the net profits of the 
Company, calculated in accordance with Section 198 of 
the Companies Act, 2013.
 The aggregate commission payable to all Managing 
Directors of the Company shall be within the overall 
limit of 10% of the net profits of the Company, calculated 
in accordance with Section 198 of the Companies Act, 
2013.
 The remuneration including commission payable to 
Mr. Pradeep Kumar Jain shall be subject to an overall 
ceiling of ` 40 crore payable to all Managing Directors of 
the Company, as may be determined by the Nomination 
and Remuneration Committee and/or the Board of 
Directors.
 Overall Ceiling:
 Total Managerial Remuneration payable to Mr. 
Pradeep Kumar Jain in his capacity as Chairman & 
Managing Director of the Company for each financial 
year, whether paid by way of Salary, Commission, 
=== page 4 ===
Notice3Notice (Contd.)
Perquisites, allowances or otherwise, shall be subject 
to the limits prescribed under Sections 196 and 197 of 
the Companies Act, 2013 read with Schedule V thereto 
and other applicable provisions of the Companies Act, 
2013, as amended from time to time. 
- The remuneration payable to Mr. Pradeep Kumar 
Jain, shall not exceed 5% of the net profits of the 
Company for any financial year, calculated in 
accordance with Section 198 of the Companies 
Act, 2013; and
- The aggregate managerial remuneration payable 
to all Managing Directors and Whole-time 
Directors of the Company shall not exceed 10% 
of the net profits of the Company for any financial 
year, calculated in accordance with Section 198 of 
the Companies Act, 2013.
- Notwithstanding the above statutory limits, 
the aggregate remuneration payable to all the 
Managing Directors of the Company shall also 
be subject to an overall ceiling of `40 crore, 
as approved by the Members of the Company, 
which ceiling shall apply exclusively to Managing 
Directors and shall be determined by the 
Nomination and Remuneration Committee and/
or the Board of Directors of the Company in 
accordance with applicable provisions of the 
Companies Act, 2013.
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT remuneration including 
salary, commission, perquisites, and other allowances 
payable to Mr. Pradeep Kumar Jain shall be increased up 
to 10% per annum during the tenure of his appointment, 
as may be determined by the Nomination and 
Remuneration Committee and/or Board of Directors 
of the Company subject to applicable provisions of the 
Companies Act, 2013.
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT in the event of loss or 
inadequacy of profits in any financial year during 
the tenure of services of Mr. Pradeep Kumar Jain 
as Chairman & Managing Director, the payment of 
managerial remuneration shall be governed by the 
limits as specified under Schedule V of the Companies 
Act, 2013 or under any other applicable provisions of 
the Companies Act, 2013 as may be amended from 
time to time or any statutory re-enactment thereof for 
the time being in force.”

NOTES
1. AGM through VC or OAVM:  In accordance with the 
provisions of the Companies Act, 2013 read with the 
General Circular No. 14/2020 dated April 8, 2020 , 
General Circular No. 17/2020 dated April 13, 2020  
and General Circular No. 20/2020 dated May 5, 
2020,  and subsequent circulars issued in this regard, 
issued by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (“MCA”), 
and General Circular No. 03/2025 dated September 
22, 2025 , whereby the MCA has permitted companies 
to continue to hold Annual General Meetings (“AGMs”) 
and Extraordinary General Meetings (“EGMs”) 
through Video Conferencing (“VC”) or Other Audio 
Visual Means (“OAVM”) until further orders, without 
the physical presence of Members at a common 
venue, and in accordance with the Circulars (as 
amended from time to time) issued by the Securities 
and Exchange Board of India (“SEBI”) read with the 
applicable provisions of the SEBI (Listing Obligations 
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 
(“SEBI Listing Regulations”), the 27th Annual General 
Meeting (“AGM”)  of the Company is being convened 
through VC/OAVM. The aforesaid MCA Circulars 
and SEBI Circulars, inter alia, permit the dispatch of 
the Notice of the AGM and the Annual Report only 
by electronic mode to those Members whose e-mail 
addresses are registered with the Company/Registrar 
and Share Transfer Agent/Depositories and dispense 
with the requirement of physical attendance of 
Members and appointment of proxies at such AGM. 
Accordingly, Members can attend and participate in 
the AGM only through VC/OAVM. In compliance with the provisions of the Companies 
Act, 2013 (“the Act”), SEBI (Listing Obligations and 
Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (“SEBI 
Listing Regulations”) and MCA Circulars and SEBI 
Circulars, the 27th Annual General Meeting (“Meeting” 
or “AGM”) of the Company is being held through VC/
OAVM on Wednesday, September 30, 2026 at 11:30 
A.M. (IST) . 
 The proceedings of AGM deemed to be conducted at 
the Registered Office of the Company situated at NBCC 
Plaza, Tower-II, 4th Floor, Pushp Vihar, Sector-V, Saket, 
New Delhi-110017.
 The detailed procedure for participation in the meeting 
through VC/OAVM is as per note no. 19 and available at 
the Company’s website www.pncinfratech.com .
2. Explanatory Statement: The Explanatory Statement 
pursuant to Section 102 of the Companies Act, 2013, 
setting out the material facts concerning item no. 5 to 
13 to be transacted at the Meeting is annexed hereto 
and forms part of the Notice.
3. Instruction for Members regarding Proxy(s): Pursuant 
to the provisions of the Companies Act, 2013, a Member 
entitled to attend and vote at the Annual General Meeting 
is entitled to appoint a proxy to attend and vote on his/
her behalf and the proxy need not be a Member of the 
Company. Since this AGM is being held pursuant to the 
MCA Circulars through VC/OAVM, physical attendance 
of Members has been dispensed with. Accordingly, the 
facility for appointment of proxies by the Members will 
not be available for the Annual General Meeting and 
hence the Proxy Form, Attendance Slip and Route Map 
are not annexed to the Notice.

12. IEPF :  The MCA had notified provisions relating to 
unpaid/unclaimed dividend under Sections 124 and 
125 of the Companies Act, 2013 and Investor Education 
and Protection Fund (Accounting, Audit, Transfer and 
Refund) Rules, 2016. As per these Rules, dividends 
which are not encashed / claimed by the shareholder 
for a period of seven consecutive years shall be 
transferred to the Investor Education and Protection 
Fund (IEPF) Authority.
 The IEPF Rules mandate the companies to transfer the 
shares of shareholders whose dividends remain unpaid 
/unclaimed for a period of seven consecutive years 
to the demat account of IEPF Authority. Hence, the 
Company urges all the shareholders to encash / claim 
their respective dividend during the prescribed period. 
The details of the unpaid / unclaimed dividends lying 
with the Company are also available on the website of 
the Company.
 During the year, the Company credited and paid Unpaid/
Unclaimed dividend amount to the Investor Education 
and Protection Fund (IEPF) for the FY 2018-19,

non-availability of the details of the bank account, 
the Company shall dispatch the dividend warrants/ 
demand drafts/ cheques to such shareholder by 
post.
vi) In terms of the provisions of the Income Tax Act, 
1961 (“the Act”), as amended by the Finance 
Act, 2020, mandates that dividends paid or 
distributed by a company is taxable in the hands 
of shareholders. The Company shall, therefore, 
be required to deduct tax at source (‘TDS’) at the 
time of making the payment of final dividend at 
the applicable tax rates. The rate of TDS would 
depend upon the category and residential status 
of shareholders and the documents submitted 
and duly accepted by the Company.
 As it is important for the Company to receive the 
relevant information/ documents from shareholders to 
determine the rate of tax deduction, the shareholders 
are requested to furnish relevant documentation in the 
prescribed manner. 
 Accordingly, shareholders are hereby requested to visit 
the Company’s website at https://www.pncinfratech.
com/financials.html  wherein the Company have 
provided complete information relating to TDS on 
dividend along with the draft of the relevant documents 
to be submitted by the shareholders.Notice (Contd.)

19. Voting through electronic means (Remote E-Voting or 
E-Voting): 
 In compliance with Section 108 of the Companies Act, 
2013, Rule 20 of the Companies (Management and 
Administration) Amendment Rules, 2015, Secretarial 
Standard-2 on General Meeting and Regulation 
44 of the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure 
Requirements) Regulations, 2015, the Company is 
pleased to provide its members facility to exercise their 
right to vote on resolutions proposed to be considered 
at the 27thAnnual General Meeting (AGM) by electronic 
means and the business may be transacted through 
e-Voting Services. The facility of casting the votes by 
the members using an electronic voting system from a 
place other than venue of the AGM (“remote e-voting”) 
will be provided by MUFG Intime India Private Limited 
(MUFG Intime).THE INSTRUCTIONS FOR SHAREHOLDERS FOR REMOTE-
VOTING ARE AS FOLLOWS:
In terms of SEBI circular no. SEBI/HO/CFD/PoD2/CIR/P/0155 
dated November 11, 2024, Individual shareholders holding 
securities in demat mode are allowed to vote through their 
demat account maintained with Depositories and Depository 
Participants. 
Shareholders are advised to update their mobile number 
and email Id correctly in their demat accounts to access 
remote e-Voting facility. 
Login method for Individual shareholders holding securities 
in demat mode:
Individual Shareholders holding securities in demat mode 
with NSDL
METHOD 1 - NSDL OTP based login
a) Visit URL: https://eservices.nsdl.com/SecureWeb/
evoting/evotinglogin.jsp  
b) Enter your 8 - character DP ID, 8 - digit Client Id, PAN, 
Verification code and generate OTP . 
c) Enter the OTP received on your registered email ID/ 
mobile number and click on login. 
d) Post successful authentication, you will be re-directed 
to NSDL depository website wherein you will be able to 
see e-Voting services under Value added services. Click

EXPLANATORY STATEMENT IN RESPECT OF THE 
ORDINARY/ SPECIAL BUSINESS PURSUANT TO 
PROVISIONS OF THE COMPANIES ACT , 2013 (“THE ACT”)
As required by provisions of the Companies Act, 2013, the 
following Explanatory Statements sets out the material 
facts relating to the Ordinary/ Special Businesses mentioned 
under Item Nos. 5 to 13 of the accompanying Notice:
Item No. 5
Section 139 of the Companies Act, 2013, lays down the 
criteria for appointment and mandatory rotation of statutory 
auditors. Pursuant to section 139 of the act and rules made 
there under, the present Statutory Auditors of the Company, 
M/s. NSBP & Company, Chartered Accountants seek for re-
appointment. Since 01st Term of the M/s. NSBP & Company, 
Chartered Accountants, (Registration No. 001075N) 
concluded at the 27th Annual General Meeting that is from 
01st April, 2021 to 31st March, 2026.
The Audit Committee and the Board of Directors of the 
Company had recommended the re-appointment of M/s. 
NSBP & Company, Chartered Accountants, New Delhi, 
Firm Registration No. 001075N (“NSBP”) as the Statutory 
Auditors of the Company. The proposed appointment is for 
a term of two consecutive years from the conclusion of 27th 
AGM till the conclusion of the 29th AGM on payment of such 
remuneration as may be mutually agreed upon between the 
Board of Directors and the Statutory Auditors, from time 
to time. There is no material change in the remuneration 
proposed to be paid to NSBP for the statutory audit to be 
conducted for the financial year ending 31st March, 2027 vis-
à-vis the remuneration paid to NSBP , the retiring Statutory 
Auditors, for the statutory audit conducted for the financial 
year ended 31st March, 2026. The proposed remuneration 
to be paid to the Auditors for the FY 2026-27 is ` 55 Lakhs 
(Rupees Fifty-Five Lakhs Only). The said remuneration 
excludes applicable taxes and out of pocket expenses. The 
Board of Directors and the audit committee shall approve 
revisions to the remuneration of the statutory auditors for 
the remaining part of the tenure.
The Board of Directors, in consultation with the audit 
committee, may alter and vary the terms and conditions of 
appointment, including remuneration, in such manner and 
to such extent as may be mutually agreed with the statutory 
auditors. NSBP has consented to their appointment as 
Statutory Auditors and have confirmed that to the Company 
that their appointment, if made, shall comply of Section 139 
and 141 of the Companies Act, 2013 and Companies (Audit 
and Auditors) Rules, 2014. 
M/s. NSBP & Co. is a firm of Chartered Accountants registered 
and established with the Institute of Chartered Accountants 
of India (ICAI). It was established in India with more than 45 years of existence and is a firm incorporated in India. It 
has its registered office at 325, 3rd Floor, U.S. Complex New 
Delhi. It is primarily engaged in providing Auditing, Assurance 
and Consulting as its core business lines for domestic and 
global businesses of all sizes. The firm has engaged a panel 
of experts and offers end-to-end solutions for Company 
Law Matters, IFRS Convergence, Taxation and Transaction 
Advisory. It is amongst the largest and highly reputed audit 
firms in India and are auditors for several large companies 
including some of the top 1000 listed entities in India.
The Audit Committee considered various parameters like 
capability to serve a diverse and complex business landscape 
as that of the Company, audit experience in the Company’s 
operating segments, market standing of the firm, clientele 
served, technical knowledge etc., and found NSBP to be 
best suited to handle the scale, diversity and complexity 
associated with the audit of the financial statements of the 
Company.
None of the Directors, Key Managerial Personnel or their 
relatives except to the extent of their shareholding, to 
whom the resolution relates, is interested in or concerned, 
financially or otherwise, in passing the proposed resolution. 
The Board recommends the resolution set forth in item no. 5 
for the approval of members
Item No. 6
The Board of Directors of the Company on recommendation 
made by Audit Committee, has approved the appointment 
of M/s. Gaurav Jain & Associates, Cost Accountant (Firm 
Registration Number - 004160) as the Cost Auditor of the 
Company for conducting the audit of the cost records and 
statements of the Company for the Financial Year 2026-27 
with remuneration of INR 1,00,000/- per annum plus taxes, 
as applicable, and reimbursement of actual out-of-pocket 
expenses incurred by them in connection with the aforesaid 
audit.
In accordance with the provisions of Section 148 of the 
Act read with the Companies (Audit and Auditors) Rules, 
2014, the remuneration payable to the Cost Auditors as 
recommended by the Audit Committee and approved by the 
Board, has to be ratified by the members of the Company.
Accordingly, ratification by the members is sought to the 
remuneration payable to the Cost Auditors for the financial 
year ending March 31, 2027 by passing an Ordinary 
Resolution as set out at Item No. 6 of the Notice.
None of the Directors / Key Managerial Personnel of the 
Company / their relatives are, in any way, concerned or 
interested, financially or otherwise, in the resolution.
The Board recommends the Ordinary Resolution set out at 
Item No. 6 of this Notice for approval of the Members.

PNC INFRATECH LIMITED Annual Report 2025-2620
Item No. 7
The Board of Directors of the Company had appointed 
Mr. Pradeep Kumar Jain, (DIN: 00086653), as Chairman & 
Managing Director on the Board of Directors for a period 
of 5 years with effect from October 1, 2021 till September 
30, 2026, which was approved by the shareholders in the 
Annual General Meeting held on September 29, 2021. Since 
the tenure of appointed Mr. Pradeep Kumar Jain, (DIN: 
00086653) as Managing Director will expire on September 
30, 2026, Mr. Pradeep Kumar Jain is proposed to be re-
appointed as the Managing Director for a further period of 
5 years. 
Based on the evaluation of performance for the previous  
term and recommendation by the Nomination and 
Remuneration Committee of the Company, the Board 
of Directors at its meeting held on August 08, 2026 
re-appointed Mr. Pradeep Kumar Jain, as a Chairman 
& Managing Director, subject to the approval of the 
shareholders, w.e.f. October 1, 2026 till September 30, 2031 
for a period of 5 years at the remuneration recommended 
by the Nomination and Remuneration Committee and 
approved by the Board as set out in the Resolution above.
Pursuant to Regulation 17(1C) of the SEBI (LODR) 
Regulations, 2015, approval of the shareholders for Re-
appointment of a person on the Board of Directors is to be 
taken at the next general meeting or within a period of 3 
months from the date of appointment whichever is earlier. 
Therefore, it is proposed to seek the member’s approval 
for the Re-appointment of and remuneration payable to  
Mr. Pradeep Kumar Jain, as a Chairman & Managing Director 
of the Company, in terms of the applicable provisions of the 
Act. 
In the opinion of the Board of Directors, he fulfils the 
condition specified in the Act for such reappointment 
and his re-appointment is in the interest of the Company. 
Mr. Pradeep Kumar Jain will attain the age of 70 years on 
August 27, 2027 and in order to continue his directorship, a 
Special Resolution has to be passed. The Board proposes 
to reappoint and retain him as the Managing Director of the 
Company in view of his vast knowledge, rich experience, and 
significant contributions to the Company's growth since 
its incorporation. Under his leadership, the Company has 
achieved substantial operational and financial progress. 
Considering his proven leadership, strategic vision, 
extensive industry expertise, and the need for continuity in 
management, the Board believes that his reappointment is in 
the best interests of the Company and its stakeholders and 
recommends the special resolution for continuation of his 
directorship in the Company post August 27, 2027 in order 
to comply with the provisions of Sections 196, 197, 198, and 
203 read with schedule V and other applicable provisions of the Companies Act, 2013, and the rules made thereunder

BROADENING 
PERSPECTIVES. 
UNLOCKING NEW 
POTENTIAL.
India’s infrastructure requirements and equally 
opportunities are widening. Roads and Highways 
sector remains as one of the core and central 
sectors to the country’s infrastructure development 
and economic growth. PNC Infratech will continue 
to pursue more business prospects in this sector.  
With sustained Government focus 
across other core infrastructure 
sectors including high-growth  
areas like railways, metro rail, water-
resource initiatives like Jal Jeevan 
Mission 2.0, power transmission, 
renewable energy and battery 
storage etc., provide robust long-
term opportunities for financially 
credible developers and construction 
companies like PNC Infratech Limited. 
While the Company has actively been 
involved for the past four years in the 
water resources development space 
including irrigation and drinking 
water supply projects, in FY 2025-26 
it has entered into coal mining and 
renewable energy with integrated 
battery storage segments; each of 
these draws on proven capabilities, 
core competencies, and financial discipline the Company already 
holds, having consistently nurtured 
them over the past three decades. 
In FY 2025-26, the Company divested 
twelve fund-based road assets, 
utilizing the proceeds to significantly 
strengthen its balance sheet and 
reduce overall leverage to pursue 
accretive growth opportunities going 
forward.
Broadening Perspectives - 
Unlocking New Potential reflects the 
Company’s current approach as it 
has widened the range of sectors it 
operates in, diligently looking for new 
avenues for sustainable growth while 
harnessing its execution strength, 
enhanced investment capabilities 
gained through capital recycling and 
financial prudence.

=== page 36 ===
PNC Infratech Limited (‘PNC Infratech’, ‘PNC Infra’, ‘PNC’ or 
‘The Company’) is an integrated infrastructure investment, 
development, construction and management company. 
Over more than 25 years of incorporation, it has built roads 
and highways, water supply, canals and irrigation systems, 
railways, airport infrastructure, industrial areas and power 
transmission networks, and has recently entered renewable 
energy and coal mining. The Company operated across 16 
states and holds road assets on PPP modes through their 
operating life, post construction.Corporate Snapshot 
The Company delivers complex 
infrastructure projects across 
India through in-house design and 
engineering, its own construction 
teams, project management 
expertise and an equipment base 
of over `1,450 Cr. That execution 
strength is what allows it to enter 
new infrastructure segments  
without loosening its hold on the 
core businesses.

Clients

PNC Infratech works almost entirely for the public sector, with the National Highways 
Authority of India, the Airports Authority of India, state road development corporations and 
central public sector enterprises among its major clients. Several have awarded the Company 
repeat contracts across more than two decades, which is the clearest measure of the 
consistency and reliability Company brings to large-scale execution.

Chairman & Managing Director's Message
REALIZING VALUE. 
REDEFINING 
HORIZONS.
DEAR STAKEHOLDERS,
In FY 2025-26, we strategically unlocked 
capital from select operational assets 
to fund our next phase of calibrated 
diversification and expansion.
Pradeep Kumar Jain
Chairman and Managing DirectorI am honored to present Annual Report for FY 2025-26  
for our Company, a year marked by transformative 
developments particularly in terms of strategic divestment 
and calibrated diversification.    
While our Company’s core strength remains rooted 
in project execution, from concept to commissioning 
with demonstrated competency in execution of roads, 
highways, bridges, flyovers and airport pavements. 
Over the years, our Company has steadily entered into 
other sectors like industrial area development, power 
transmission, irrigation and drinking water supply and 
successfully delivered nearly 100 major projects across 
sectors and geographies. 
With proven capabilities and deep expertise in 
construction, the Company expanded into the 
development space nearly two decades ago in 2007. 
It began investing in public-private partnership (PPP) 
projects across a variety of delivery formats including 
Build-Operate-Transfer (BOT)-Toll, BOT-Annuity, and 
Hybrid Annuity Mode (HAM), focusing primarily on the 
highway sector. 
As part of the strategy for calibrated diversification, in 
FY 2025-26, the Company successfully entered the coal 
mining and solar power with battery energy storage 
sectors. These new sectors create new avenues for 
growth while leveraging the deep operational capabilities 
we have developed over more than three decades.
In FY 2025-26, our Company undertook a key 
transformative move and achieved a defining milestone 
and executed through successful divestment of twelve 
operational, fund-based highway assets. This portfolio 
comprised 11 HAM national highway projects and 1 
BOT-Toll state highway project. The capital unlocked 
from these monetized assets substantially strengthened 
our balance sheet through deleveraging while equipping 
the Company with enhanced financial agility to pursue a 
higher volume of larger-scale opportunities in the fund-
based segment.  
12
PNC INFRATECH LIMITED Annual Report 2025-26Annual Report 2025-26
=== page 45 ===
Macro-Economic Scenario 
India’s economic momentum remains exceptionally strong, 
demonstrating remarkable resilience amid mounting 
geopolitical tensions and headwinds. Despite intensifying 
rivalries among major powers, ongoing conflicts in Ukraine 
and the Middle East, trade disputes, and sanctions-driven 
supply chain disruptions, the nation continues to navigate 
global volatility effectively. Even as climate change and 
resource scarcity compound these pressures, India 
has achieved impressive GDP growth, underscoring its 
underlying macroeconomic stability. 
The Union Budget for FY 2027 underscores the 
Government’s commitment to accelerating infrastructure 
growth, allocating approximately `2.9 trillion to the roads 
and highway sector, an 8% year-on-year increase as the 
roads and highways sector continues to be one of the 
focused infrastructure sectors.  
Industry Landscape
The roads and highways sector experienced a subdued 
awarding environment during FY 2025-26 also, with 
project awards by the National Highways Authority of 
India (NHAI) remaining below the annual target. NHAI 
awarded 3,124 km of projects, a shortfall of more than 
30%, against the annual target of 4,500 km. Delays in 
land acquisition and project approvals also held back 
the commencement of several projects. The persistent 
slowdown in project awarding, combined with delays in 
declaring appointed dates for already awarded contracts, 
adversely impacted project execution schedules and 
overall operational revenues throughout the year under 
review. Navigating these challenging dynamics reinforced 
the importance of maintaining strict execution discipline 
while simultaneously developing new avenues for long-
term and sustainable growth. 
India’s fundamental infrastructure landscape remains 
exceptionally robust, driven by sustained central and state 
initiatives and investments across transport, water, energy, and urban development. Beyond highways, emerging 
PPP and HAM-led frameworks in railways promise 
new synergies for experienced developers. A planned 
`6.0 lakh Cr capital expenditure in power transmission 
over the next five financial years will facilitate power 
evacuation toward targeted non-fossil capacities of 
500 GW by 2030 and 900 GW by FY36, catalysing vast 
opportunities in transmission, energy storage, and 
renewable infrastructure. Concurrently, major momentum 
in railways, airport development, and water projects, 
evidenced by the Jal Jeevan Mission reaching 82% rural 
tap coverage by July 2026 underscores a multi-sector 
expansion phase. Supported by this robust, long-term 
project pipeline, well-capitalized companies possessing 
proven execution expertise, financial credibility, and 
cross-sector agility like our Company are uniquely 
positioned to capture this next wave of national growth.
Standalone Performance and Strategic Milestones
Against this backdrop, our standalone performance 
reflected the acute constraints faced by the Company in 
project commencement. Standalone revenue for  
FY 2025-26 stood at `4,633 Cr, down from the previous 
year largely due to delay in receiving appointed dates. 
Standalone EBITDA was `583 Cr with an EBITDA margin 
of 12.58%. Standalone PAT for FY26 was `344 Cr with a 
PAT margin of 7.43%. Lower execution volumes weighed 
on EBIDTA and profitability to certain extent, and our 
response was to hold cost discipline and strengthen the 
platform for the next phase of growth. 
The Company received `235 Cr under the Vivad 
Se Vishwas III scheme in settlement of the Agra 
Bypass arbitration matter with NHAI. Together, 
these developments strengthened our liquidity and 
financial flexibility, giving us greater capacity to pursue 
opportunities while maintaining the financial discipline 
that has always been central to our approach.
Financial Statements03
Statutory Reports02
Corporate Overview01
13
=== page 46 ===
Our established capabilities in project 
planning, engineering, raw material sourcing, 
equipment deployment and construction 
continue to support the delivery of complex 
projects across geographies. 
Operational Excellence and Execution Prowess
While our Company broadens its business landscape, 
efficient and timely execution will remain at the centre 
of our strategy. Our established capabilities in project 
planning, engineering, raw material sourcing, equipment 
deployment and construction continue to support the 
delivery of complex projects across geographies. During 
the year, we achieved PCOD for the Mathura 1B and 
Prayagraj-Kaushambi Package 3 HAM projects, alongside 
other execution milestones.
Our order book provides a strong base for the next phase 
of growth, while our integrated resources, including 
our equipment fleet and access to key construction 
materials, support timely mobilization and execution. The 
Company’s unexecuted order book stands at over  
`18,000 Cr as on March 31, 2026. We will continue to 
strengthen the order book as the scale and diversity of  
our project portfolio increase.
Strategy for the Future: FY 2026-27 and Beyond
Building on FY 2025-26, our strategy rests on three 
priorities, which are consolidation, calibrated sectoral 
diversification and international expansion. Together they 
will convert our project pipeline, financial strength and 
execution capabilities into consistent performance and 
sustained growth.
First, our Company will consolidate its existing platform 
by converting our project pipeline into revenue through 
timely execution, supported by the commencement of 
projects by securing appointed dates expeditiously as we 
are targeting revenue growth of around 30% in FY 2026-27 
and 25% in FY 2027-28.Second, the Company will continue to pursue calibrated 
diversification into new segments where we find 
synergies. With stronger financial position following 
capital recycling, the Company will evaluate larger  
fund-based opportunities while holding to prudent capital 
allocation and risk management.
Third, our Company has begun evaluating opportunities 
outside India. Our initial bids in Uzbekistan mark an 
initial step in this direction. We will explore international 
opportunities selectively and diligently, with the same 
emphasis on execution capability, commercial  
viability and financial discipline that guides our  
domestic operations.
Closing Thoughts
FY 2025-26 has fortified the foundation for our 
Company’s next growth phase, driven by the strategic 
recycling of capital from operational assets and targeted 
entry into new infrastructure sectors, all while maintaining 
an uncompromising focus on timely, efficient execution. 
Our clear imperative now is to translate this strong 
foundation into long-term, sustainable growth.
While the opportunities ahead are vast, PNC Infra will

house, quarries its own aggregate, runs an equipment fleet of over `1,450 Cr and builds with its 
own construction teams rather than subcontracting the critical work. That ownership is what 
holds costs and schedules where the Company sets them, across projects in eleven states.Business Model

Company Overview
Incorporated in 1999, PNC Infratech Limited has been a key contributor to India’s infrastructure development, with a strong 
focus on roads and highways. The Company provides end-to-end infrastructure execution capabilities, essentially offering 
Engineering, Procurement, and Construction (EPC) services under Design-Build turnkey contracts. It also undertakes 
projects through various public-private partnership (PPP) models, including on Design-Build-Finance-Operate-Transfer 
(DBFOT), Annuity & Hybrid Annuity Mode (HAM) and Operate-Maintain-Transfer (OMT) formats. PNC Infratech is among the 
few players in the sector with established expertise spanning investment, project development, construction and long-term 
asset management.MANAGEMENT DISCUSSION 
AND ANALYSIS
Yamuna Bridge, Mathura (Package 1B)
24
PNC INFRATECH LIMITED Annual Report 2025-26
=== page 57 ===
Leading Infrastructure Player Driven by 
Integrated In-House Competencies
The Company has built a strong reputation through 
the successful execution of a diverse project portfolio, 
including bridges, flyovers, airport pavements, roads, 
highways, expressways, industrial area development, and 
rural drinking water supply systems. 
Backed by strong technical expertise and financial 
strength, the Company is well-positioned to independently 
undertake large-scale EPC projects. Its commitment to 
infrastructure development is reflected in its investments 
over the past five years, including significant deployment 
of capital in the highway sector through a mix of equity 
and debt.
With a substantial gross block and an extensive fleet of 
construction equipment, PNC Infratech has the capacity 
to execute large-scale projects annually, demonstrating 
significant operational scalability. The Company also 
drives cost efficiency through the ownership and leasing 
of quarries, ensuring a reliable and uninterrupted supply of 
key raw materials such as stone aggregates.
Further strengthening its execution capabilities is a 
highly skilled workforce, spanning design, planning, 
engineering, construction, operation, maintenance, traffic, 
environmental engineering, viability assessment, and 
project development and management. These teams 
are supported by strong functions in accounts, taxation, 
finance, procurement, human resources, stores, safety and 
security and administration, enabling the consistent and 
efficient delivery of high-quality infrastructure solutions.Expanding Sectoral Boundaries Through 
Focused Diversification
With over two decades of technical and operational 
expertise, the Company is well-positioned to sustain 
its growth trajectory through robust risk mitigation and 
working capital optimization strategies. Its strategic 
roadmap emphasizes measured expansion into high-
potential sectors such as railways, water supply and 
irrigation and coal mining. 
While roads and highways remain the core focus, the 
Company is actively diversifying its project portfolio 
to maintain growth momentum and reduce segment 
concentration. The goal is to achieve a balanced mix 
where highways contribute approximately 60% and 
other sectors the remaining 40%. In recent years, 
this diversification has already taken shape through 
strategic foraying into mining, railways, metro rail, water 
infrastructure, transmission lines, and renewable energy 
with active participation in bids floated by authorities in 
these sectors. Notably, the Company won a Coal Mining 
project and a Solar Energy cum Battery Energy Storage 
System (BESS) project, further broadening its footprint.
Water infrastructure is expected to be a significant 
near-to-medium-term growth driver, with increasing 
participation in government-led initiatives such as the Jal 
Jeevan Mission, Water Resource Development (Irrigation 
etc.) likely to enhance the order book substantially. 
Concurrently, efforts are underway to deepen the 
Company’s presence in railway infrastructure.
Today, PNC Infra’s operations span a comprehensive 
range of infrastructure development, including BOT-Toll, 
HAM and conventional Annuity road projects, irrigation 
and water supply systems, industrial area development, 
and airport runway construction, and management/
maintenance of completed projects reflecting a well-
diversified and strategically expanding portfolio.

Cluster-Based Regional Focus with a  
Pan-India Footprint
The Company is headquartered in North India and has 
built an exceptional execution track record in the region, 
which continues to command a substantial share of its 
order book. While maintaining strong regional expertise, 
it has successfully expanded its pan-Indian footprint 
across multiple states, including Delhi-NCR, Uttarakhand, 
Karnataka, Maharashtra, Andhra Pradesh, Bihar, Uttar 
Pradesh, Haryana, Madhya Pradesh, Gujarat, and 
Chhattisgarh.
To date, the Company has executed over 100 major 
infrastructure projects across 16 states. These span a 
diverse mix with 74 road EPC projects, 23 airport runway 
works, 1 railway track construction, 2 power transmission 
projects, 1 industrial area redevelopment, and 1 water 
supply project.
On the asset-ownership front, PNC Infratech is currently 
managing 1 project under BOT-Toll, 2 under BOT-Annuity, 
and Fourteen HAM projects. 14 HAM projects are having 
an aggregate bid project cost of over `17,000 Cr 
As of March 31, 2026, 5 HAM projects have achieved 
PCOD/COD, 6 are under construction, 1 project has 
achieved financial closure, and for 2 projects in which 
Company has signed Concession Agreements with NHAI. 
In addition, the Company entered two new segments 
during FY 2025-26 viz. coal mining and a Solar Power 
cum BESS transmission project further diversifying  
its portfolio.
Uttar Pradesh has emerged as one of India’s fastest-
growing states in both urban and rural infrastructure, 
and PNC Infratech is strategically placed to capitalize 
on this accelerated development agenda. Its long-
standing presence, deep understanding of the regional 
landscape, and cluster-based project execution model 
enable efficient resource allocation, stronger operational 
synergies, and stable margins. This focused regional 
strategy allows the Company to remain cost-competitive 
while sustaining profitability. As a dominant participant 
in recent project awards across North India particularly 
in Uttar Pradesh, PNC is well-positioned for sustainable 
growth and value creation in the years ahead.
Established Pre-Qualification Credentials 
and Enduring Government Client 
Relationships
With an operational legacy spanning over two decades, 
PNC Infratech has built deep domain expertise through 
sustained collaboration with both central and state 
government agencies. The Company has long-standing 
partnerships with key infrastructure authorities, including 
the National Highways Authority of India (NHAI), the 
Ministry of Road Transport and Highways (MoRTH), 
the Airports Authority of India, Military Engineering 
Services, various State Roads Development Authorities, 
South-Eastern Coalfields, National Hydroelectric Power 
Corporation and Public Works Departments.
During FY 2025-26, PNC Infratech continued to 
demonstrate its execution resilience by strategically 
managing multiple projects across diverse geographies, 
effectively mitigating risks while optimizing working 
capital management. The Company’s consistent track 
record and proven delivery capabilities have firmly 
established its pre-qualification credentials for  
large-scale infrastructure projects. Notably, PNC Infratech 
possesses the financial and technical strength to 
independently bid for projects valued up to `10,000 Cr 
underscoring its ability to pursue large-size mandates 
without relying on consortium partnerships.
This effective combination of proven experience, healthy 
financials, trusted government relationships, and 
independent bidding capacity reinforces PNC Infratech’s 
standing as a formidable player in India’s evolving 
infrastructure landscape.Ganga Bridge, Prayagraj

Asset Monetization
In January 2024, the Company entered into definitive 
agreements with Vertis Infrastructure Trust (formerly 
Highways Infrastructure Trust), an Infrastructure 
Investment Trust (InvIT) sponsored by affiliates of 
KKR & Co. Inc. to divest 12 road assets comprising 11 
National Highway HAM assets and 1 State Highway 
BOT Toll asset, spanning an aggregate length of 
approximately 3,800 lane-km. 
The transaction was structured in two tranches. Under 
Tranche I, completed in May 2025, the Company, along 
with its Wholly Owned Subsidiary PNC Infra Holdings 
Limited, divested 10 HAM assets for an equity 
consideration of `1,827.6 Cr, subject to adjustments 
for certain descoped works. Additionally, around  
`200 Cr of other receivables since realized/to be 
realized over time from the respective SPVs, in 
accordance with the terms of the agreements.
Subsequently, in July/August 2025, the Company and 
PNC Infra Holdings Limited completed the divestment of their entire stake in the 11th asset BOT-Toll,  
PNC Bareilly Nainital Highways Private Limited, for  
an equity consideration of `153 Cr The total  
equity investment across these 11 assets stood  
at `1,446 Cr.
In March 2026, the Company completed the sale of its 
remaining asset (HAM), PNC Challakere (Karnataka) 
Highways Private Limited. The transaction was 
concluded at an enterprise value of `683.84 Cr, 
including `80.48 Cr received towards equity and  
`134.94 Cr towards unsecured loans on  
March 27, 2026.
The successful completion of this transaction marks 
a key milestone in the Company’s asset monetization 
journey, enabling it to unlock value from its  
operational portfolio, strengthen its balance sheet,  
and enhance financial flexibility to pursue future 
growth opportunities.
Financial overview
PNC is one of the few companies in the infrastructure and 
construction space in India which has posted consistent 
operating cash flow/free cash flow (OCF/FCF) post-
interest expense over FY 2013-14 to FY 2025-26.  
The Company has negligible net interest expense 
as a percentage of EBITDA, thus having significant 
distributable cash flow. It has avoided unrelated 
diversification as well as aggressive growth campaigns.
The Standalone Revenue for FY26 stood at `4,633 Cr.
The EBITDA for FY 2025-26 is `583 Cr and the Profit After 
Tax for FY 2025-26 is Rs. `344 Cr.
The Company’s Net Worth as on March 31, 2026, on a 
Standalone basis is `5,811 Cr, whereas the total debt is 
`741 Cr The Company has a net cash surplus of  
`327 Cr The debt-to-equity ratio for FY 2025-26 is 0.13x.Focused Financial Strategy Backed by a Growing Order Book
PNC Infratech’s financial strategy is rooted in prudent cost control, balanced value offering, and a progressively stronger 
balance sheet creating a solid foundation to capture emerging growth opportunities in fund-based projects. 
The Company’s financial standing remains robust, with a standalone debt-to-equity ratio of 0.13x as of FY 2025-26, 
and consolidated ratio of 0.76x. To fund future HAM projects, the focus remains on internal accruals and the calibrated 
monetization of operational assets, ensuring growth is achieved without over leveraging the balance sheet. 
This financial discipline is further supported by a healthy order book position, with the unexecuted order book standing over 
`18,000 Cr as of March 31, 2026.
The current ratio, which indicates the Company’s ability to 
pay short-term obligations, has remained strong at 2.68x 
for FY 2025-26, as compared to 2.36x for FY 2024-25.
During the year, CARE Ratings Limited has reaffirmed 
its rating to AA+ with a stable outlook for the long-term 
facilities and A1+ for the short-term bank facilities of the 
Company.
On a consolidated basis, the revenue for FY 2025-26 
stood at `5,368 Cr In terms of segment contribution, the 
Roads EPC segment contributed approximately 76%, 
Water Segment contributed 11% whereas the Toll/Annuity 
Income contributed 13% for FY 2025-26. 
The Consolidated EBITDA for FY 2025-26 is `1,137 Cr  
and the Consolidated Profit After Tax for FY 2025-26  
is `832 Cr .
The Company’s net worth as on March 31, 2026, on a 
Consolidated basis is `6,813 Cr, whereas the total debt is 
`5,151 Cr The Net Debt to Equity on a Consolidated basis 
is 0.76x.

Risks and Mitigation Mechanism
The Company faces competition from 
a wide range of local, national, and 
international players across its operating 
segments, with intensity varying by 
geography and project type. Competitive 
pressures remained elevated in  
FY 2025-26, driven by multiple 
government initiatives being smaller in 
size, including relaxed bidding norms. 
This heightened competition may 
result in aggressive bidding, potentially 
exerting pressure on contract pricing and 
operating margins.Competition Risk
Mitigation
Backed by over three decades of experience in construction 
and engineering, the Company has built a strong execution 
track record. It strategically focuses on large-sized projects 
(even over `5,000 Cr value), where competitive intensity is 
relatively lower. Supported by a robust balance sheet and 
deep domain expertise, the Company delivers integrated 
solutions spanning design, engineering, planning, and 
execution. This enables efficient delivery of complex 
projects with an emphasis on safety, timeliness, and cost 
optimization. Additionally, PNC aims to accelerate project 
completion timelines to benefit from early completion 
incentives, where applicable.
The Company’s operations are largely 
concentrated in North India, with a 
significant exposure to Uttar Pradesh. 
Such geographic concentration exposes 
it to region-specific regulatory and 
political risks, which could impact 
project execution and overall business 
performance.Geographical Concentration Risk
Mitigation
To mitigate this risk, the Company is proactively 
diversifying its presence across geographies and sectors. 
It is expanding into new states while also venturing into 
emerging segments such as irrigation, railways, metros, 
transmission lines, and renewable energy. With a proven 
track record of successful engagements with multiple 
state governments, the Company is well-placed to scale its 
operations in new regions. Additionally, it optimizes the use 
of its extensive construction equipment fleet by deploying 
resources efficiently across nearby project sites, enhancing 
operational flexibility and execution efficiency.
With increasing digitalisation of 
operations, exposure to cyber threats 
such as ransomware, phishing, and other 
malicious activities has risen significantly 
across industries. As IT systems play 
a critical role in project execution and 
the deployment of technology-driven 
solutions, any disruption could adversely 
affect operational efficiency and business 
continuity.Cyber Risk
Mitigation
While the Company has not encountered any cyber 
incidents so far, it adopts a proactive and vigilant approach 
to cybersecurity. It has implemented comprehensive 
safeguards and systems to strengthen its cyber resilience 
and continuously monitors evolving threat landscapes. 
This ongoing assessment helps ensure robust protection 
of its digital infrastructure and minimizes potential 
vulnerabilities.
28
PNC INFRATECH LIMITED Annual Report 2025-26
=== page 61 ===
The macroeconomic environment 
and infrastructure sector have been 
facing sustained inflationary pressures, 
primarily driven by rising input costs. 
Critical materials such as structural steel, 
cement, bitumen, fuel, concrete, metal 
plates, cables, and other electrical and 
mechanical components have witnessed 
notable price increases, which may 
adversely impact the Company’s cost 
structure and margins.Inflation Risk
Mitigation
The Company follows a proactive and structured approach 
to manage input cost volatility, which is critical for 
efficient project execution. It retains control over key cost 
components through ownership of strategic assets and 
long-term procurement arrangements, including its own 
stone aggregate mines and crushers, which are major 
contributors to optimize overall project costs.
Additionally, the Company sources essential raw 
materials such as cement, steel and bitumen from 
established manufacturers with whom it has long-
standing relationships, ensuring reliability in quality, timely 
availability, and competitive pricing.
To further mitigate the impact of cost fluctuations, 
the Company incorporates price-escalation clauses in 
contracts with government clients. These provisions enable 
adjustments linked to changes in input costs, thereby 
helping protect margins throughout the project lifecycle.
The risk that the Company may be unable 
to meet its short-term and long-term 
financial obligations due to potential 
mismatches in cash flows.Liquidity/Cash Flow Risk
Mitigation
The Company maintains a strong financial position with 
adequate liquidity buffers to service its debt obligations. 
It follows a disciplined approach to budgeting and closely 
tracks project-level cash flows, costs, and funding 
requirements. In addition, it actively monitors both micro 
and macroeconomic trends, enabling timely and informed 
financial decision-making.
Volatility in the prices of key construction 
inputs, particularly bitumen, fuel, steel 
and other petroleum-based materials, 
may increase project costs and 
create pressure on project margins. 
Global geopolitical developments and 
fluctuations in crude oil prices may 
further amplify input-cost volatility, 
potentially impacting cost management 
and execution profitability.Commodity Price Volatility Risk
Mitigation
The Company leverages contractual price-escalation 
mechanisms to provide protection against fluctuations 
in key input costs. For applicable HAM/EPC projects, any 
price escalation received by the SPV is passed through 
to the EPC contractor on a back-to-back basis, thereby 
limiting the Company’s exposure to commodity price 
inflation. In addition, the Company closely monitors input 
prices and procurement requirements to facilitate timely 
sourcing and support effective project cost management.

=== page 62 ===
Human Resource Management 
PNC Infratech places strong emphasis on continuous 
skill development and capability enhancement across its 
teams. The senior management is largely homegrown, 
bringing deep, grassroots-level expertise in engineering, 
procurement, project management, and execution. The 
Company also fosters a culture of accountability by 
supporting its employees through both motivational 
initiatives and performance-linked incentives.
Internal Control Systems
The Company has established robust internal control 
systems aligned with the scale and complexity of its 
operations. These systems ensure that assets are 
procured efficiently, safeguarded against unauthorized 
use or loss, and that all transactions are duly authorized, 
accurately recorded, and properly reported. The internal 
audit function strengthens these controls through well-
defined policies, procedures, and periodic reviews. Audits 
are conducted across functions as per an annual audit 
plan, with findings regularly reported to management 
and the Audit Committee. The effectiveness of internal 
controls is further validated through inputs from statutory 
and ISO auditors. Additionally, project sites are secured 
through advanced CCTV surveillance, and operations are 
supported by an integrated SAP ERP system, enabling 
continuous monitoring and improvement by management.Cautionary Statement
The Company has included forward-looking statements 
and information in this Annual Report to help investors

2. FINANCIAL PERFORMANCE 
 On Standalone Basis 
 On a standalone basis, revenue of the Company for FY 2025-26 is ` 4,63,346.96 Lakhs as compared to ` 5,51,312.38 Lakhs 
in FY 2024-25. The Operating Profit (i.e. Earnings before Interest, Tax, Depreciation and Amortization) for FY 2025-26 is  
` 58,285.68 Lakhs as compared to ` 1,04,886.4 Lakhs in FY 2024-25. The Profit before Tax for FY 2025-26 is ` 47,089.78 
Lakhs as compared to ` 94,892.17 Lakhs in FY 2024-25. The Profit after Tax for FY 2025-26 is ` 34,414.36 Lakhs as 
compared to ` 70,560.52 Lakhs in FY 2024-25.
 On Consolidated Basis
 The Consolidated Revenue of the Company for FY 2025-26 is ` 5,36,810.16 Lakhs as compared to ` 6,76,868.37 Lakhs 
in FY 2024-25. The Consolidated Operating Profit (i.e. Earnings before Interest, Tax, Depreciation and Amortization) for  
FY 2025-26 is ` 113666.99 Lakhs as compared to ` 2,06,606.08 Lakhs in FY 2024-25. The Consolidated Profit before 
Tax for FY 2025-26 is ` 1,06,663.03 Lakhs as compared to ` 1,19,156.07 Lakhs in FY 2024-25. The Consolidated Profit 
after Tax, Minority Interest and Share in Profit / Loss of Associate for FY 2025-26 is ` 83,150.49 Lakhs as compared to  
` 81,541.75 Lakhs in FY 2024-25.

 There was no change in the nature of the business during the financial year 2025-26.

1. A brief outline on CSR Policy of the Company are as follows:
a. Promoting education among children and women.
b. Promoting Healthcare, Eradicating Hunger, Poverty and Malnutrition, and making available safe drinking water.
c. Animal Welfare & Agro Forestry.
d. Empowering Women.
e. Promoting education among children and women.
f. Promoting Art & Culture
g. Rural Development.

 The CSR activities are aligned to the specified activities in Schedule VII of the Companies Act, 2013. The above may be 
modified from time to time, as per recommendations of the CSR Committee/approval of Board of the Company.

3. Provide the web-link where Composition of CSR committee, CSR Policy and CSR projects approved by the board are 
disclosed on the website of the Company: i.e. www.pncinfratech.com
4. Provide the executive summary along with web-link(s) of Impact Assessment of CSR Projects carried out in pursuance of 
sub-rule (3) of rule 8, if applicable.
 Below is the executive summary of the impact assessment of CSR projects carried out in pursuance of sub-rule (3) of rule 
8 of the Companies (Corporate Social Responsibility Policy) Rules, 2014. The detailed impact assessment report(s) can be 
accessed on the website of the Company https://www.pncinfratech.com/financials.html
 A team of consultants and professionals from De Jure Services has independently carried out the impact assessment. The 
aim was to measure the impact created by CSR investments by the Organization. To this end, the evaluation team assessed 
all the CSR activities undertaken by the Company across the different region(s), in the areas of Animal Welfare & Agro 
Forestry, Promoting Healthcare, Poverty and Malnutrition Reduction, Women empowerment, Facilities for senior citizens 
(including old age homes) and Promoting education. The financial reports and administrative data for the evaluation were 
provided by the Company and survey(s) has been done from beneficiaries and stakeholders.
 The methodology for the assessments included 3 major components:
•	 Qualitative Methods; 
•	 Quantitative Methods;
•	 Mixed Methods; 
 The information gathered through all the above methods was collated and the findings were carved up in order to assess 
the impact created by the different projects undertaken by the organization.
 The impact reports present key information like objective of the project undertaken, the impact created by the organization, 
end-user satisfaction and sustainability measures. The evaluation of the CSR projects highlights the contributions made 
towards achieving their objectives as well as assessment of sustainability and recommendations for improvement.

c.  A brief on types of customers
 PNC Infratech Limited is a leading integrated infrastructure solutions provider with over 25 years of experience, 
innovation and excellence across the infrastructure value chain. The Company possesses proven capabilities 
spanning investment, design, development, construction, operation, maintenance and management of large-scale 
infrastructure projects. With a strong track record of executing complex projects, PNC Infratech has established 
robust proven execution capabilities and operational excellence across diverse infrastructure segments.
The Company has built a strong geographical footprint across India and has diversified its operations across 
various segments, including Coal Mining; Roads & Highways EPC; Roads & Highways BOT, OMT and HAM; Water 
Supply & Irrigation; Industrial Area Development; Airport Runways; Power Transmission; and Dedicated Freight 
Corridors and Railways. This diversified portfolio enables the Company to leverage opportunities across multiple 
infrastructure domains while strengthening the resilience of its revenue streams.
PNC Infratech undertakes projects through multiple implementation models, including Item Rate contracts, 
Engineering Procurement and Construction (EPC)/Design-Build, Build-Operate-Transfer (BOT) under Toll and 
Annuity formats, Operation, Maintenance and Transfer (OMT), and Hybrid Annuity Mode (HAM). The Company’s 
diversified capabilities, established execution track record and strong order book position it well to participate in 
the development of India’s growing infrastructure ecosystem.
The Company has built a strong and diversified clientele, comprising Central and State Government agencies, 
statutory authorities and leading infrastructure development organisations. Its key clients include the National 
Highways Authority of India (NHAI), Ministry of Road Transport & Highways (MoRTH), Airports Authority of 
India (AAI), Bihar State Road Development Corporation Limited (BSRDC), Maharashtra State Road Development 
Corporation Limited (MSRDC), Madhya Pradesh Road Development Corporation Limited (MPRDC), City and 
Industrial Development Corporation of Maharashtra Limited (CIDCO) and other government and infrastructure 
authorities. Through its established client relationships, diversified portfolio and execution expertise, PNC 
Infratech continues to contribute to the development of critical infrastructure across India.

has underscored the necessity to 
enhance the resilience of urban 
regions. Many infrastructures and 
public areas weren’t planned to cope 
with the environmental challenges we 
currently face, including severe weather

and water scarcity. Tackling climate 
change demands the expansion of 
innovative solutions in areas such 
as energy, efficiency, material use, 
and beyond. Government policies are 
catalyzing a shift towards eco-friendly 
construction. The climate action agenda 
presents commercial opportunities that 
empower us to aid in climate change 
adaptation and reduction.
Potential alterations brought on by 
climate change, such as regulatory 
modifications, shifts in consumer 
preferences, and other substantial 
changes in the Company’s operational 
context, could also significantly affect 
the business and poses a risk.As an organization, we 
recognize the imperative to

ConditionsR Companies need to ensure that they are 
not indirectly involved in human rights 
abuses. Violations like poor working 
conditions, modern slavery, child labor,

locations/sites or by subcontractors 
or suppliers in our supply chain can 
inflict harm on individuals and the 
environment.
Such transgressions can lead to 
reputational damage, a loss of trust in 
the Company’s responsibility, and can 
result in fines, penalties, civil litigation, 
and criminal prosecution. It is essential 
for businesses to respect and support

and the effective recognition of the right

businesses must actively work towards

8.Water 
usage and 
managementR/O Risk:
Water plays a vital role in construction 
activities and has significant

throughout its extraction, usage, 
and discharge processes. Given the 
growing strain on water resources and 
the imperative to protect the natural 
environment, effective control and 
management of water resources have 
become crucial. Thus, it is essential to 
encourage the responsible and efficient 
use of water, along with the development 
of infrastructure that promotes its 
conservation in a sustainable and 
environmentally-friendly manner. 
Identifying and mitigating water 
related risks, as well as implementing 
responsible management procedures, 
are necessary steps towards achieving 
these goals.
Opportunity:
Implementing effective water usage 
and management practices not only 
reduces costs but also mitigates 
the adverse effects of groundwater 
depletion and water pollution.
By optimizing water utilization,

while simultaneously minimizing 
their contribution to the depletion of

10.  Health and safety management system
a.  Whether an occupational health and safety management system has been implemented by the entity? (Yes/ No)  
Yes 
 If Yes, the Coverage such systems?
 PNC has established an Occupational Health and Safety Management System across its operations to support the 
health, safety, and well-being of its employees. The Company undertakes periodic safety inspections and internal 
audits to identify potential hazards and assess associated risks. Based on these assessments, appropriate preventive 
and corrective measures are implemented to mitigate identified risks and maintain a safe and secure workplace.
b.  What are the processes used to identify work-related hazards and assess risks on a routine and non-routine basis 
by the entity?
 The Company has established a structured risk management framework to identify, assess, and mitigate potential 
hazards across its construction sites, manufacturing units, industrial facilities, and office premises. Project engineers, 
supervisors, and other relevant personnel are actively engaged in monitoring and managing identified risks. This 
collaborative approach facilitates comprehensive risk management and strengthens the Company’s efforts towards 
maintaining a safe and secure working environment.

PNC INFRATECH LIMITED Annual Report 2025-26PRINCIPLE 4: Businesses should respect the interests of and be responsive to all its stakeholders.
(This principle highlights the importance of stakeholder engagement. Companies should consider the interests and perspectives 
of all stakeholders, including shareholders, employees, customers, suppliers, and the communities in which they operate. They 
should also be responsive to stakeholder concerns and feedback.)
Essential Indicators
1.  Describe the processes for identifying key stakeholder groups of the entity.
 PNC Infratech identifies its stakeholders as individuals or groups that can influence the Company’s ability to achieve its 
objectives or are affected by its operations and activities. These stakeholders play an important role in shaping, and are 
in turn impacted by, the Company’s strategic decisions and business operations. The stakeholder identification process 
is undertaken by senior management in consultation with the Board of Directors and relevant internal departments. 
Stakeholders are identified and prioritised based on their relevance to the Company and the nature and extent of their 
potential impact on, or interaction with, its operations. The process encompasses both internal and external stakeholder 
groups, many of whom engage with the Company regularly through formal and informal channels, including day-to-day 
interactions. Key stakeholder groups include employees, workers, investors, customers, suppliers, contractors, government 
and regulatory authorities, and local communities, among others that are integral to PNC Infratech’s business ecosystem.

PNC INFRATECH LIMITED Annual Report 2025-268.  Mechanisms to prevent adverse consequences to the complainant in discrimination and harassment cases.
 The Company has established appropriate policies and procedures to protect the rights and interests of individuals 
raising concerns, including safeguards against discrimination, harassment, or any form of retaliation. A Vigil Mechanism 
(Whistleblower Policy) is in place to provide employees and directors with a secure and confidential platform to report bona 
fide concerns relating to unethical practices, suspected fraud, violations of the Code of Conduct, or non-compliance with 
Company policies.
 The mechanism enables individuals to raise concerns in good faith without fear of adverse consequences or retaliation, 
thereby supporting a workplace culture founded on transparency, accountability, and trust.

10.  Briefly describe the waste management practices adopted in your establishments. Describe the strategy adopted by 
your company to reduce usage of hazardous and toxic chemicals in your products and processes and the practices 
adopted to manage such wastes
 Hazardous waste and chemicals are managed and stored in accordance with the applicable requirements of the Hazardous 
and Other Wastes (Management and Transboundary Movement) Rules, 2016 and the Manufacture, Storage and Import of 
Hazardous Chemicals Rules, 1989.
 Hazardous waste and chemicals are segregated from other waste streams and stored in designated areas using 
appropriately sealed and labelled containers to minimize the risk of leakage or spillage. Wherever feasible, such waste 
is reused at project sites or transferred to authorized vendors for recycling and appropriate disposal. Employees are also 
provided with necessary guidance and awareness on the safe handling, storage, and management of hazardous waste and 
chemicals.

5.  Does the entity have a business continuity and disaster management plan? (Yes/No)
 Yes
 Give details in 100 words/ web link.
 The Company operates multiple project sites and ensures that isolated incidents do not impact the project schedule by 
building in sufficient buffer time. The Company also revises operational procedures wherever practical to prevent issues 
at one location from affecting others. Additionally, in case of any disaster or force majeure condition at one locations, the 
Company has other locations from where the work and business can continue. The Company has a policy of making the 
employees aware of these procedures so that in consultation with the management, critical processes can be quickly 
restored and work can resume without significant impact.

PNC INFRATECH LIMITED Annual Report 2025-26Report on Corporate Governance
Your Company is committed to attain the highest standard 
of Corporate Governance. The Company’s Corporate 
Governance structure plays a pivotal role in realizing this 
long term goal. It provides the fundamental systems, 
processes and principles that promote objective decision 
making, performance based management and a corporate 
culture that is characterized by integrity and fairness in all 
dealings. This practice is affected by attempts to align the 
interests of stakeholders of the Company. Your Company 
considers Corporate Governance is all about maintaining a 
valuable relationship and trust with all its stakeholders and 
considers stakeholders as partners in its success and will 
remain committed to maximizing its stakeholder’s value.
Company’s Philosophy
Your Company’s philosophy in relation to Corporate 
Governance is to ensure transparent disclosures and 
reporting that confirms full compliance to the applicable laws, regulations and guidelines, and to promote ethical 
conduct of the business operation with primary objective of 
enhancing all stakeholders’ value while being a responsible 
corporate citizen. Your Company is committed to meet 
the aspirations of all stakeholders. Your Company firmly 
believes that any meaningful policy on the Corporate 
Governance must provide empowerment to the executive 
management of the Company and simultaneously create a 
mechanism of checks and balances which ensures that the 
decision making power vested in the executive management 
are used with care and responsibility to meet shareholders 
and stakeholders expectations. This is demonstrated 
in shareholders returns, high credit ratings, recognition 
from Authorities, governance processes and focussed 
work environment. Your company not only adheres to the 
prescribed Corporate Governance practices as per the SEBI 
Listing Regulations but also committed to sound Corporate 
Governance principles and practices. 
Your Board functions through Board of Directors and various 
committees constituted to oversee specific operational 
areas with a professional management team below board 
level. Your Company undertakes to take all necessary 
steps to comply with Code of Corporate Governance on a 
continuous basis.
Your Directors present the Company’s Report on Corporate 
Governance in compliance with the Regulation 34(3) read 
with Schedule V of SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure 
Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (hereinafter referred to as 
SEBI Listing Regulations) for the FY 2025-26.

 The Board of Directors of your Company has an 
ideal combination of Executive and Non-Executive 
Directors and is in conformity with the provisions 
of Companies Act, 2013 and Regulation 17 of 
the SEBI Listing Regulations, which inter alia 
stipulates that the Board should have an optimum 
combination of Executive and Non-Executive 
Directors with at least one Woman Director and 
not less than 50% of the Board should consist 
of Independent Directors if the chairman of the 
Company is an Executive Director.

PNC INFRATECH LIMITED Annual Report 2025-26e)  Information to the Board
 A detailed agenda was sent to each Director 
via email, seven days in advance of the Board 
Meetings. As a policy, all major decisions involving 
investments and loans, in addition to matters 
which statutorily require the approval of the Board 
are put up for consideration of the Board. All the 
agenda items are backed by necessary supporting 
information and documents (except for the critical 
price sensitive information, which is circulated 
separately or placed at the meeting) to enable the 
Board to take informed decisions.
 The Board periodically reviews compliance status 
of laws applicable to the Company, prepared by 
the Management. Further, the Board also reviews 
the Annual financial statements of the Unlisted 
Subsidiary Companies. In addition to the above, 
pursuant to the Regulation 24 of the SEBI Listing 
Regulations, the Minutes of the Board Meetings 
of the Company’s Unlisted Subsidiary Companies 
and a statement of all significant transactions 
and arrangements entered into by the Unlisted 
Subsidiary Companies are placed before the 
Board.
 Recording Minutes of Proceedings at Board and 
Committee Meetings
 The Company Secretary records minutes of 
proceedings of each Board and Committee 
meetings. Draft minutes are circulated to Board/ 
Committee members for their comments within 
the stipulated time period. The minutes are 
entered in the Minutes Book within 30 days from 
the conclusion of the meeting.
 Post Meeting Follow-Up Mechanism
 The guidelines for Board and Committee meetings 
facilitate an effective post meeting follow-up, review and reporting process for decisions taken 
by the Board and Committees thereof.
 Important decisions taken at Board/ Committee 
meetings are communicated promptly to the 
concerned departments/divisions. Action-taken 
report on decisions/ minutes of the previous 
meeting(s) is placed at the succeeding meeting of 
the Board/Committees for the noting.

i)  Confirmation from Board regarding fulfilment of 
the independence criteria by IDs
 In the opinion of the Board, the Board confirms that 
the Independent Directors of the Company fulfil the 
conditions specified in SEBI (LODR) Regulations, 
2015 and are independent of the management. 
The Board is also in opinion that there needs to 
be continuous assessment of the independence 
criteria. Regulatory requirements for testing the 
independence of Directors are currently based 
on factual information or checklists. However, 
true independence is a function of behaviour, 
and an objectiveness being brought to board 
deliberations and overall decision making. This is 
already discussed in sub point c of this report.
 Meeting of Independent Directors
 Regulation 25 of Listing Regulations and Schedule 
IV of the Act, read with the Rules thereunder 
mandate that the Independent Directors of the 
Company shall hold at least one meeting in a 
financial year, without the presence of Non-
Independent Directors and members of the 
Management.
 At such meeting the Independent Directors 
discuss, among other matters, the performance 
of the Company and risk faced by it, the flow 
of information to the Board, competition, 
strategy, leadership strengths and weaknesses, governance, compliance, Board movements and 
performance of the executive members of the 
Board, including the Chairman.
 The Committees and the Board are updated by the 
Independent Directors about the outcome of the 
meetings and actions, if any, required to be taken 
by the Company.
 During FY 2025-26, the Independent Directors 
met without the presence of management on 06 
February, 2026 and March 30, 2026 chaired by Mr. 
Gauri Shankar.
